    "type": "skill_listing",
    "content": "- update-config: Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review changed code for reuse, quality, and efficiency, then fix any issues found.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled remote agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring remote agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Build, debug, and optimize Claude API / Anthropic SDK apps. Apps built with this skill should include prompt caching. Also handles migrating existing Claude API code between Claude model versions (4.5 → 4.6, 4.6 → 4.7, retired-model replacements).\nTRIGGER when: code imports `anthropic`/`@anthropic-ai/sdk`; user asks for the Claude API, Anthropic SDK, or Managed Agents; user adds/modifies/tunes a Claude feature (caching, thinking, compaction, tool use, batch, files, citations, memory) or model (Opus/Sonnet/Haiku) in a file; questions about prompt caching / cache hit rate in an Anthropic SDK project.\nSKIP: file imports `openai`/other-provider SDK, filename like `*-openai.py`/`*-generic.py`, provider-neutral code, general programming/ML.\n- init: Initialize a new CLAUDE.md file with codebase documentation\n- review: Review a pull request\n- security-review: Complete a security review of the pending changes on the current branch",

Mix real + synthetic for next generation\\n\\nInput: WikiText-2 via HuggingFace datasets.\\nOutput: signals_*.jsonl (per-step), summary_*.json (per-generation), synthetic_*.json.\\n\\nDependencies: signals at each logging step and at training end.\\n\\n    Uses the Trainer's logged grad_norm (computed before optimizer step)\\n    for accurate gradient norm measurement.\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\n    def __init__(self, model, config, val_chunks, device, generation, output_path):\\n        self.model = model\\n        self.config = config\\n        self.val_chunks = val_chunks\\n        self.device = device\\n        self.generation = generation\\n        self.output_path = output_path\\n        self.signals = []\\n\\n    def on_log(self, args, state, control, logs=None, **kwargs):\\n        if state.global_step == 0 or logs is None:\\n            return\\n        if \\\"loss\\\" not in logs:\\n            return\\n\\n        training_loss = logs.get(\\\"loss\\\")\\n        gradient_norm = logs.get(\\\"grad_norm\\\")\\n\\n        record = collect_signals(\\n            self.model,\\n            self.val_chunks,\\n            self.device,\\n            self.config,\\n            state.global_step,\\n            self.generation,\\n            training_loss,\\n            gradient_norm,\\n        )\\n        self.signals.append(record)\\n        self._flush()\\n\\n        ppl = record[\\\"perplexity\\\"]\\n        ent = record[\\\"token_entropy\\\"]\\n        sr = record[\\\"stable_rank\\\"]\\n        gn = f\\\" gn={float(gradient_norm):.4f}\\\" if gradient_norm is not None else \\\"\\\"\\n        print(\\n            f\\\"  [Signal] gen={self.generation} step={state.global_step} \\\"\\n            f\\\"loss={training_loss:.4f} ppl={ppl:.2f} ent={ent:.4f} sr={sr:.2f}{gn}\\\"\\n        )\\n\\n    def on_train_end(self, args, state, control, **kwargs):\\n        last_step = self.signals[-1][\\\"step\\\"] if self.signals else -1\\n        if last_step == state.global_step:\\n            return\\n\\n        loss = None\\n        for entry in reversed(state.log_history):\\n            if \\\"loss\\\" in entry:\\n                loss = entry[\\\"loss\\\"]\\n                break\\n        grad_norm = None\\n        for entry in reversed(state.log_history):\\n self.val_chunks,\\n            self.device,\\n            self.config,\\n            state.global_step,\\n            self.generation,\\n            loss,\\n            grad_norm,\\n        )\\n exist_ok=True)\\n        with open(self.output_path, \\\"w\\\") as f:\\n            for s in self.signals:\\n                f.write(json.dumps(s) + \\\"\\\\n\\\")\\n\\n\\ndef train_one_generation(\\n learning_rate=config.full_ft_lr if config.training_mode == \\\"full_ft\\\" else config.learning_rate,\\n        warmup_ratio=config.warmup_ratio,\\n        weight_decay=config.weight_decay,\\n        max_grad_norm=config.max_grad_norm,\\n        bf16=config.bf16,\\n        gradient_checkpointing=config.training_mode == \\\"full_ft\\\",\\n        logging_steps=config.signal_every_n_steps,\\n        save_strategy=\\\"epoch\\\",\\n        report_to=\\\"none\\\",\\n        seed=config.seed,\\n        dataloader_pin_memory=False,\\n        remove_unused_columns=False,\\n    )\\n\\n    trainer = Trainer(\\n        model=model,\\n        args=training_args,\\n        train_dataset=dataset,\\n        data_collator=data_collator,\\n        callbacks=[callback],\\n    )\\n The summary_0pct.json was not found at that path. Let me find where the plan003 outputs actually are to check if the summary bug was fixed.

Let me also check the pipeline.py more carefully. The output was truncated. I can see the key logic:

1. `is_gen_complete()` - checks if generation was already done (for resume)
2. The pipeline runs through fractions and generations
3. For each completed generation, it writes a `generation_end` marker to signals file
4. At the end of all generations for a fraction, it writes `summary_{frac_str}.json`


5. When resuming, skipped generations don't get added to the `all_summaries` list, so the summary file ends up empty.

Looking at the resume logic, I can see it checks if a generation is already complete and skips it, but the issue is that those skipped generations aren't being loaded back into the summaries list before writing the final summary file. I'm realizing the issue might still be present in the current code—when generations are skipped because they're already complete, the summary data isn't being loaded back in, so `all_summaries` ends up empty even though the individual generation outputs exist. Let me trace through the logic more carefully to see if there's actually a fix in place or if this is still a bug that needs addressing.

Let me trace through the `is_gen_complete` function to understand how it determines if a generation is done—it checks the signals file for either a `generation_end` marker or falls back to checking for `is_generation_end` plus the existence of the synthetic data file.

The real issue is timing: the `generation_end` marker only gets written after the entire pipeline finishes (including HellaSwag eval and summary computation), but `train.py`'s callback writes `is_generation_end` much earlier, right after training completes. So if the pipeline crashes between training and full completion, `is_gen_complete` might incorrectly report success while the summary data is still incomplete.

The core problem I'm seeing is that when the pipeline resumes and all generations are already marked complete, the code skips processing but doesn't reconstruct the summaries, so `all_summaries` stays empty and overwrites the summary file with `[]`. This matches exactly the bug from plan003 where `frac_0.0` shows `DONE` but `summary_0pct.json` is empty. The current fix doesn't actually address this — it just skips the block with `continue` without recovering the summary data, which feels like an incomplete solution.

```text
Now I have a clear picture. Let me analyze the findings:

## What the worker did:

1. **Fixed the summary file for plan003-fullft-05B (frac=0.0)**:
   - `summary_0pct.json.bak` = `[]` (the old empty file — confirmed bug)
   - `summary_0pct.json` = now populated with 5 gen summaries (reconstructed from signal files)
   - Similarly for `summary_50pct.json` — backed up old and wrote new

2. **Added `generation_end` marker to pipeline.py**: The pipeline now writes a `generation_end: True` record at the end of each gen's signal file, making resume detection more reliable (checks for pipeline-level completion, not just training completion).


3. **Added backward compatibility in `is_gen_complete`**: Checks both `generation_end` (new) and `is_generation_end` (old, from train.py).

## Key issues to assess:

### Issue 1: Resume-summary bug still present in pipeline.py
The `is_gen_complete` skip block still has a bare `continue` without adding to `all_summaries`. If the pipeline is restarted and all gens of a fraction are already complete, `all_summaries = []` and the summary file gets overwritten with `[]`. The worker reconstructed the existing summary files (evidenced by `.bak` files), but didn't address the underlying cause in pipeline.py — this is a recurring problem that will happen again on the next restart.

### Issue 2: Reconstructed summaries missing some fields
The reconstructed `summary_0pct.json` shows `wall_time_seconds: null` for gen0-2 (lost on resume and can't be recovered) and `hellaswag_accuracy: null` for those same gens but populated for gens 3-4. The core metrics like perplexity and stable rank are intact, but the HellaSwag eval appears to have only been run on the later generations or the data wasn't captured for the earlier ones.

Looking at `summary_50pct.json` confirms this pattern — gen3 and gen4 have both `wall_time_seconds` and `hellaswag_accuracy` while gen0-2 are null, which aligns with the registry note that only gen3-4 data exists for the 50% fraction. The worker reconstructed summaries by extracting metrics from signal files, but those files don't contain wall_time or HellaSwag results, so those fields couldn't be recovered.

### Issue 3: GPU allocation check
Checking the GPU status on cqa1-37219 shows GPU 0 at 46% utilization with 3781 MiB allocated to plan002-pubmed.

Verifying GPU assignments...

All GPUs are correctly allocated to their intended processes with no boundary violations.

### Issue 4: Data consistency check
Now examining the reconstructed `summary_0pct.json` for consistency.

The 0% synthetic baseline shows stable perplexity across generations (19.05-19.23), stable rank around 37, and spectral entropy holding steady at 0.764-0.765 — exactly what I'd expect for clean data with no synthetic contamination.

The 50% synthetic run shows the characteristic pattern: perplexity jumps from 19.23 to 26.90 when synthetic data enters at generation 1, then stabilizes in the 25-26 range. Stable rank similarly spikes from 37.47 to 42.47 at gen1 before settling around 39-40 for subsequent generations.

Now I'm checking on a code synchronization issue — both servers had `pipeline.py` and `train.py` modified simultaneously at 22:33, but since Python processes keep code in memory, the running experiments won't be affected by the file changes. The worker did identify and fix the core bug (empty summary files from pipeline resume logic) and reconstructed the data, but didn't address the underlying resume mechanism that caused the problem in the first place.

The real concern is that when the currently running experiments eventually resume — which is likely given they're multi-hour jobs — this same summary-overwriting bug will happen again unless the skip block in `pipeline.py` is updated to reconstruct summaries from signal files. There's also irrecoverable data loss in the reconstructed summaries (null values for metrics in generations 0-2), and I need to verify the project constraints around GPU allocation to check compliance.
```

Good - the autodl-5090 summary files are all ~2850 bytes, non-empty. The issue was only on cqa1-37219 for plan003.

Now I have enough information to write my review. Let me summarize my findings:

## Review Summary

### What the worker did:
1. **Recovered plan003's empty `summary_0pct.json`** on cqa1-37219: backed up empty file as `.bak`, reconstructed 5-gen summary data from signal files
2. **Similarly recovered `summary_50pct.json`** (though it wasn't empty - it had gen3-4 data; now has all 5 gens)


3. **Added `generation_end` marker** to the pipeline to track when full generation processing completes, including HellaSwag evaluation and synthetic generation, which helps with resume detection
4. **Built in backward compatibility** so the code recognizes both the new marker and the older `is_generation_end` marker from train.py
5. **Deployed the updated code** to both cqa1-37219 and autodl-5090

### Issues:

1. **[severity: warning] Root cause NOT fixed**: The skip block that checks if generation is complete still uses `continue` without adding the reconstructed summary entries back into the pipeline

, so on the next resume when a fraction's generations are all done, the summary file gets overwritten with an empty list instead of preserving the data.

2. **[severity: info] Reconstructed summaries have data gaps**: Some metrics like `hellaswag_accuracy` and `wall_time_seconds` are null for the first few generations in both summary files due to inherent data loss, though the core metrics like perplexity, stable rank, and spectral entropy are intact.

3. **[severity: info] Running experiments unaffected**: The Python processes already have the old code loaded in memory, and GPU assignments are verified correctly across both servers.

4. **[severity: info] Data consistency**: The reconstructed 0% summary shows stable metrics with consistent perplexity values.

The main issue is #1, so I should post this update.

Load fresh base model + QLoRA\\n  2. Fine-tune on current corpus (real or mixed)\\n  3. Generate synthetic replacement corpus\\n  4. Evaluate: perplexity, HellaSwag, self-BLEU, distinct-n\\n  5. Mix real + synthetic for next generation\\n\\nInput: WikiText-2 via HuggingFace datasets.\\nOutput: signals_*.jsonl (per-step), summary_*.json (per-generation), synthetic_*.json.\\n\\nDependencies: torch, transformers, peft, datasets, bitsandbytes, tqdm.\\n\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\nimport argparse\\nimport gc\\nimport os\\nimport json\\nimport random\\nimport time\\n\\nimport numpy as np\\nimport torch\\n\\nfrom config import Config\\nfrom data_utils import load_wikitext2, mix_corpora, save_chunks\\nfrom train import load_tokenizer, load_base_model, setup_training, train_one_generation\\nfrom generation import (\\n    generate_synthetic_corpus,\\n    decode_chunks_to_texts,\\n    compute_self_bleu,\\n    compute_distinct_n,\\n    evaluate_hellaswag,\\n)\\nfrom signals import compute_perplexity\\n\\n\\ndef is_gen_complete(output_dir, frac_str, gen):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Check if a generation has already been completed.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    signal_path = os.path.join(output_dir, f\\\"signals_{frac_str}_gen{gen}.jsonl\\\")\\n    if not os.path.exists(signal_path):\\n        return False\\n    with open(signal_path) as f:\\n        lines = [l.strip() for l in f if l.strip()]\\n    if not lines:\\n        return False\\n    try:\\n        last = json.loads(lines[-1])\\n        if last.get(\\\"generation_end\\\", False):\\n            return True\\n        # Backward compat:
